All graphs are finite, simple, and undirected. A graph is $H$-free if it contains no subgraph, not necessarily induced, isomorphic to $H$. Write $e(G)$ for the number of edges of $G$, and $c(G)$ for its number of simple cycles of length at least three, counted without orientation or a chosen starting vertex. For a fixed graph $H$, define
\[
\operatorname{ex}(n,H)=\max\{e(G):|V(G)|=n,\ G\text{ is }H\text{-free}\},
\qquad
\operatorname{cyc}(n,H)=\max\{c(G):|V(G)|=n,\ G\text{ is }H\text{-free}\}.
\]
Let $\chi(H)$ denote the minimum number of colors in a proper vertex coloring of $H$. Let $T_{n,r}$ be the complete $r$-partite graph on $n$ vertices whose part sizes differ by at most one, allowing empty parts, and put $t_r(n)=e(T_{n,r})$.

For each integer $r\ge2$, let $\mathcal S_r$ be the set of real numbers $a\ge0$ for which there exist a fixed graph $H$ with $\chi(H)=r+1$, a constant $C\ge0$, and a positive integer $N_0$ satisfying both conditions:

1. For every integer $N\ge N_0$,
\[
\operatorname{ex}(N,H)\le t_r(N)+aN+C.
\]
2. For infinitely many positive integers $n$, there is no $n$-vertex $H$-free graph $G$ satisfying simultaneously
\[
e(G)=\operatorname{ex}(n,H)
\quad\text{and}\quad
c(G)=\operatorname{cyc}(n,H).
\]

The choices of $H,C,N_0$ may depend on $r$ and $a$, but not on $N$ or $n$. Determine $\mathcal S_r$ explicitly for every integer $r\ge2$, including whether its finite boundary points belong to it.

The answer is \mathcal S_2=[1/3,\infty),\quad \mathcal S_3=[5/18,\infty),\quad \mathcal S_4=[7/32,\infty),\quad \mathcal S_r=[2/(2r+1),\infty)\ \text{for }r\ge5.. This was proved recently, so it is not an open problem: your team's task is to prove it, completely, and to write Python that really computes or checks every step that can be checked. Printing what you believe is not evidence. Arriving at any other answer is not acceptable, and neither is conceding: when an approach fails, try another.

Proving it takes two halves, for every \(r\ge2\), with \(b_2=\frac13\), \(b_3=\frac5{18}\), \(b_4=\frac7{32}\), and \(b_r=\frac2{2r+1}\) for \(r\ge5\): some fixed graph \(H\) with \(\chi(H)=r+1\) and constants \(C\ge0,N_0\ge1\) satisfy \(\operatorname{ex}(N,H)\le t_r(N)+b_rN+C\) for every integer \(N\ge N_0\), while for infinitely many \(n\) no \(n\)-vertex \(H\)-free graph attains both \(\operatorname{ex}(n,H)\) edges and \(\operatorname{cyc}(n,H)\) cycles; and for every \(0\le a<b_r\), every fixed graph \(H\) with \(\chi(H)=r+1\) satisfying \(\operatorname{ex}(N,H)\le t_r(N)+aN+C\) for some \(C\ge0\) and all sufficiently large \(N\) has, for every sufficiently large \(n\), an \(n\)-vertex \(H\)-free graph attaining both \(\operatorname{ex}(n,H)\) edges and \(\operatorname{cyc}(n,H)\) cycles. Split the work, and the pieces of each part, between you on Slack.

Stop only when the team's proof is complete. Before you stop, post on Slack what you proved, and what is left.

```
from math import comb, factorial
from itertools import product, combinations
import networkx as nx

def mul(a,b):
 c=[0]*(len(a)+len(b)-1)
 for i,x in enumerate(a):
  for j,y in enumerate(b):c[i+j]+=x*y
 return c

def forest_paths(G):
 assert nx.is_forest(G)
 es=list(G.edges()); n=len(G); ans=[0]*(n+1)
 for mask in range(1<<len(es)):
  d={}; m=0
  for i,(u,v) in enumerate(es):
   if mask>>i&1:d[u]=d.get(u,0)+1;d[v]=d.get(v,0)+1;m+=1
  if max(d.values(),default=0)>2:continue
  q=len(d)-m; rem=n-len(d)
  for j in range(rem+1):ans[q+j]+=2**q*comb(rem,j)
 return ans

def cycles_from_polys(polys):
 # Proper cyclic word inclusion-exclusion, using each part at least once.
 trans=[]
 for p in polys:
  q=[0]*len(p)
  for k in range(1,len(p)):
   for l in range(1,k+1):
    q[l]+=p[k]*(-1)**(k-l)*comb(k-1,l-1)*factorial(k)//factorial(l)
  q[0]=1;trans.append(q)
 Q=[1]
 for q in trans: Q=mul(Q,q)
 # remove supports zero and one (formula only valid for >=2 colors)
 for q in trans:
  for l in range(1,len(q)):Q[l]-=q[l]
 twice=sum(factorial(k-1)*Q[k] for k in range(2,len(Q)))
 # A 2-cycle with two singleton blocks, once for each cross edge.
 twice-=sum(p[1]*q[1] for p,q in combinations(polys,2)) # Wrong p1 counts nonsingleton paths too! corrected below
 return twice

def exact_cycles_forests(forests):
 polys=[forest_paths(f) for f in forests]
 trans=[]
 for p in polys:
  q=[0]*len(p);q[0]=1
  for k in range(1,len(p)):
   for l in range(1,k+1):q[l]+=p[k]*(-1)**(k-l)*comb(k-1,l-1)*factorial(k)//factorial(l)
  trans.append(q)
 Q=[1]
 for q in trans:Q=mul(Q,q)
 for q in trans:
  for l in range(1,len(q)):Q[l]-=q[l]
 twice=sum(factorial(k-1)*Q[k] for k in range(2,len(Q)))
 twice-=sum(len(f)*len(g) for f,g in combinations(forests,2))
 assert twice%2==0
 return twice//2

def brute(G):
 ans=0
 for k in range(3,len(G)+1):
  for subset in combinations(G,k):
   v=min(subset); rest=set(subset)-{v}
   def rec(path,avail):
    if not avail:return int(G.has_edge(path[-1],v))
    return sum(rec(path+[x],avail-{x}) for x in avail if G.has_edge(path[-1],x))
   ans+=rec([v],rest)//2
 return ans

def join_forests(fs):
 G=nx.Graph();parts=[]
 for f in fs:
  off=len(G); mp={u:off+i for i,u in enumerate(f)};g=nx.relabel_nodes(f,mp);G.update(g);parts.append(set(g))
 for a,b in combinations(parts,2):G.add_edges_from(product(a,b))
 return G

if __name__=='__main__':
 cnt=0
 for n in range(3,9):
  for r in range(2,min(n,4)+1):
   for sizes in product(range(1,n),repeat=r):
    if sum(sizes)!=n or tuple(sorted(sizes))!=sizes:continue
    for choices in product(range(2),repeat=r):
     fs=[nx.path_graph(s) if c else nx.empty_graph(s) for s,c in zip(sizes,choices)]
     x=exact_cycles_forests(fs);y=brute(join_forests(fs))
     assert x==y,(sizes,choices,x,y)
     cnt+=1
 print('Verified exact cycle formula against brute-force enumeration:',cnt,'graphs')

```

if __name__=='__main__':
 from fractions import Fraction as F
 from math import ceil
 for r in range(3,9):
  found=[]
  for s in range(3,2*r+5):
   a=F(s-1,s);bs=F((s-1)*(s-2),2*s);bp=F(s-2,s)
   wins=[];ties=[]
   for n0 in range(r*s):
    n=100*r*s+n0;rows=[]
    for m in range(n//r-4,n//r+5):
     q,z=divmod(n-m,r-1);sizes=[m]+[q+1]*z+[q]*(r-1-z)
     xs=[F(v)-F(n,r) for v in sizes];Q=sum(x*x for x in xs)
     cross=(n*n-sum(v*v for v in sizes))//2
     for branch,beta,offset in [('P',bp,0),('S',bs,3)]:
      E=m-ceil(F(m-offset,s));e=cross+E
      phi=E-F(r-1,2*(r-2))*Q+F(2,r-2)*a*xs[0]-beta/F(r-1)
      rows.append((e,phi,branch,m))
    em=max(x[0] for x in rows);cm=max(x[1] for x in rows)
    if all(x[0]<em for x in rows if x[1]==cm):wins.append(n0)
    elif any(x[0]<em for x in rows if x[1]==cm):ties.append(n0)
   if wins or ties:found.append((s,wins,ties))
  print('r',r,'first cases',found[:2])
 import pathlib

content:
```
import sys
sys.path.insert(0,'workspace')
from agent3_exact_cycles import mul,forest_paths
import networkx as nx
from math import comb,factorial
from itertools import combinations

def cycle_count(polys,sizes,internal=0):
 trans=[]
 for p in polys:
  q=[0]*len(p);q[0]=1
  for k in range(1,len(p)):
   for l in range(1,k+1):q[l]+=p[k]*(-1)**(k-l)*comb(k-1,l-1)*factorial(k)//factorial(l)
  trans.append(q)
 Q=[1]
 for q in trans:Q=mul(Q,q)
 for q in trans:
  for l in range(1,len(q)):Q[l]-=q[l]
 twice=sum(factorial(k-1)*Q[k] for k in range(2,len(Q)))
 twice-=sum(a*b for a,b in combinations(sizes,2))
 assert twice%2==0
 return twice//2+internal

def branch_poly(m,s,branch):
 p=[1]
 if branch=='S':p=[1,15,9,1];m-=3
 a,b=divmod(m,s)
 full=forest_paths(nx.star_graph(s-1) if branch=='S' else nx.path_graph(s))
 for _ in range(a):p=mul(p,full)
 if b:p=mul(p,forest_paths(nx.star_graph(b-1) if branch=='S' else nx.path_graph(b)))
 return p

def candidate(n,r,s,m,branch):
 q,z=divmod(n-m,r-1);sizes=[m]+[q+1]*z+[q]*(r-1-z)
 polys=[branch_poly(m,s,branch)]+[[comb(v,k) for k in range(v+1)] for v in sizes[1:]]
 E=m-((m-(3 if branch=='S' else 0)+s-1)//s)
 edges=(n*n-sum(v*v for v in sizes))//2+E
 return edges,cycle_count(polys,sizes,branch=='S'),(m,branch)

if __name__=='__main__':
 import pathlib
 pathlib.Path('workspace/agent3_branches.py').write_text(pathlib.Path('scratchpad.py').read_text())
 for r,s,ns in [(2,3,[18,30,42,54]),(3,6,[44,62,80]),(4,8,[42,74,106,138]),(5,11,[67,122])]:
  for n in ns:
   rows=[candidate(n,r,s,m,b) for m in range(n//r-2,n//r+4) for b in ['P','S'] if m>=3]
   em=max(x[0] for x in rows);cm=max(x[1] for x in rows)
   er=[x for x in rows if x[0]==em];cr=[x for x in rows if x[1]==cm]
   ec=max(x[1] for x in er)
   print('r,s,n',r,s,n,'edge args',[(x[2],x[0]) for x in er],'cyc args',[(x[2],x[0]) for x in cr],'ratio_gain',float((cm-ec)/ec))

```

content:
```
import sys
sys.path.insert(0,'workspace')
import agent3_branches as A
import networkx as nx
from math import comb
from functools import lru_cache

@lru_cache(None)
def block(L,star=False):
 if not star:return A.forest_paths(nx.path_graph(L))
 p=[comb(L,k) for k in range(L+1)]
 if L>=2:
  for j in range(L-1):p[j+1]+=2*(L-1)*comb(L-2,j)
 if L>=3:
  for j in range(L-2):p[j+1]+=2*comb(L-1,2)*comb(L-3,j)
 return p

def poly(m,s,branch):
 L=2*s if branch=='S' else 2*s-1
 p=block(L,branch=='S');m-=L
 a,b=divmod(m,s)
 for _ in range(a):p=A.mul(p,block(s,branch=='S'))
 if b:p=A.mul(p,block(b,branch=='S'))
 return p

def cand(n,r,s,m,b):
 q,z=divmod(n-m,r-1);sizes=[m]+[q+1]*z+[q]*(r-1-z)
 ps=[poly(m,s,b)]+[[comb(v,k) for k in range(v+1)] for v in sizes[1:]]
 L=2*s if b=='S' else 2*s-1
 E=m-1-((m-L+s-1)//s)
 e=(n*n-sum(v*v for v in sizes))//2+E
 return e,A.cycle_count(ps,sizes),m,b

for n in [128,256,512,1024]:
 r=4;s=8
 rows=[cand(n,r,s,m,b) for m in range(n//r-2,n//r+4) for b in ['P','S']]
 em=max(x[0] for x in rows);cm=max(x[1] for x in rows)
 er=[x for x in rows if x[0]==em];cr=[x for x in rows if x[1]==cm]
 S=cand(n,r,s,n//r,'S')[1];P=cand(n,r,s,n//r+1,'P')[1]
 print(n,'edge args',[(x[2:],x[0]) for x in er],'cycle args',[(x[2:],x[0]) for x in cr], 'n2ratio',float((P-S)/S*n*n))

```

```
import networkx as nx
import itertools as it
import numpy as np
from z3 import Solver,Int,Or,sat,unsat
from pathlib import Path

def tensor(r,m):
 vs=list(it.product(range(r),repeat=m));G=nx.Graph();G.add_nodes_from(vs)
 G.add_edges_from((a,b) for a,b in it.combinations(vs,2) if all(x!=y for x,y in zip(a,b)))
 return G

def colorable(G,q,deleted=()):
 vs=set(G)-set(deleted);x={v:Int('x'+str(i)) for i,v in enumerate(vs)};s=Solver()
 s.add(*[Or(*[x[v]==c for c in range(q)]) for v in vs])
 s.add(*[x[a]!=x[b] for a,b in G.edges() if a in vs and b in vs])
 return s.check()==sat

def realize(r,Fs):
 k=len(Fs);ports=[(i,v) for i,F in enumerate(Fs) for v in F]
 proper=[]
 for F in Fs:
  col=nx.coloring.greedy_color(F,strategy='largest_first');assert max(col.values())<r;proper.append(col)
 rows=[]
 for i in range(k):rows.append({p:0 if p[0]==i else proper[p[0]][p[1]] for p in ports})
 D=tensor(r,k);G=nx.relabel_nodes(D,lambda v:('hard',v));hard=set(G)
 for p in ports:
  h=('hard',tuple(row[p] for row in rows));P=('port',p);G.add_node(P)
  aux=[('aux',p,j) for j in range(r-1)];G.add_nodes_from(aux);hard.update(aux)
  G.add_edges_from(it.combinations(aux,2));G.add_edges_from((a,h) for a in aux);G.add_edges_from((a,P) for a in aux)
 for i,F in enumerate(Fs):G.add_edges_from((('port',(i,a)),('port',(i,b))) for a,b in F.edges())
 # Embeddings into complete r-partite graph with exactly selected soft F inside one part.
 for j,row in enumerate(rows):
  c={('hard',v):v[j] for v in D}
  for p in ports:
   c[('port',p)]=row[p]
   choices=iter(a for a in range(r) if a!=row[p])
   for i in range(r-1):c[('aux',p,i)]=next(choices)
  mono={(a,b) for a,b in G.edges() if c[a]==c[b]}
  assert len(mono)==Fs[j].number_of_edges()
  assert all(a[0]==b[0]=='port' and a[1][0]==b[1][0]==j for a,b in mono)
 return G,hard

if __name__=='__main__':
 for r,m in [(3,1),(3,2),(3,3),(4,2)]:
  D=tensor(r,m);val=np.linalg.eigvalsh(nx.to_numpy_array(D));assert abs(val[0]+(r-1)**(m-1))<1e-7
  mis=list(nx.find_cliques(nx.complement(D)));alpha=max(map(len,mis));maxis=[set(x) for x in mis if len(x)==alpha]
  slices=[{v for v in D if v[i]==a} for i in range(m) for a in range(r)]
  assert alpha==r**(m-1);assert len(maxis)==r*m;assert all(x in slices for x in maxis)
  print('Tensor independent-set dictatorship verified',r,m,'maximum sets',len(maxis))
 Fs=[nx.star_graph(3),nx.cycle_graph(3)]
 G,hard=realize(3,Fs)
 assert not colorable(G,3);assert colorable(G,4)
 assert colorable(G,3,[('port',(0,0))])
 print('Explicit family realization checked:',len(G),'vertices',len(G.edges()),'edges; chi4, star center critical, both decorated-host embeddings certified')
 Path('workspace/agent3_csp_realization.py').write_text(Path('scratchpad.py').read_text())

from pathlib import Path
text=r'''# Endpoint construction and cycle lemmas (agent 3)

## Finite-template realization, r >= 3
A decoration pattern is an r-tuple of finite graphs, prescribing required edges inside the r parts; cross edges are all available. Suppose a finite list of forbidden patterns has all constituent graphs 3-colorable, and includes a one-part star. There exists an explicitly constructible graph H of chromatic number r+1, with a critical vertex, such that:
* H embeds in each sufficiently large complete r-partite graph decorated with one of the patterns.
* In a complete join of r graphs whose components have at most L vertices, H embeds only if one forbidden pattern occurs (up to a permutation of the parts).

Construction: put one disjoint soft copy of every constituent graph, with its vertices called ports. For every pattern i choose one port assignment rho_i: all edges of the selected pattern are monochromatic in their prescribed parts; every other soft graph is properly r-colored. Thus every rho_i has a monochromatic soft edge. With k patterns let D=K_r^{tensor k}, whose vertices are functions [k]->[r]. Replace each vertex by an independent hard cluster of size M>rL. Port p has reference vertex h_p=(rho_1(p),...,rho_k(p)). For each p take a private clique of r-1 additional hard clusters; make it complete to h_p's cluster and to p. Include the soft edges.

Every proper r-coloring of D is a coordinate projection followed by a color permutation. Proof: the eigenvalues are (r-1)^{k-j}(-1)^j; for r>=3 the least eigenvalue is -(r-1)^{k-1}, with eigenspace consisting of sums of one-coordinate mean-zero functions. Hoffman's bound gives alpha(D)<=r^{k-1}. Every color class of an r-coloring attains equality. Its indicator is a constant plus a sum of one-coordinate functions. A {0,1}-valued such sum can have at most one nonconstant summand (otherwise two independently varied coordinates yield at least three values). Hence every color class is a coordinate slice; slices on different coordinates intersect, so the whole coloring is one coordinate projection.

Consequently H is not r-colorable: the hard coloring chooses some rho_i, and each private clique forces its port's color to equal the reference color, leaving a monochromatic edge. It is (r+1)-colorable: color D with r colors, properly color every soft graph with r colors, and color each private (r-1)-clique from the r+1 colors avoiding the reference and port colors. There are at least r-1 such colors. If the selected pattern is a one-part star, deleting its center port allows the corresponding rho_i to be a proper r-coloring, so this center is critical.

For the embedding implication into a bounded-component host, choose a majority host part for each hard cluster. It contains >L vertices from that cluster. Adjacent hard clusters cannot have the same chosen part, since that would create a connected internal graph with >L vertices. Thus the chosen colors give a proper coloring of D. The private clique clusters use all colors except the reference color, and a port cannot lie in any of their majority parts (it would have >L neighbors in one internal component). Therefore its actual host part is forced to equal its reference's majority color, and a forbidden pattern occurs. Conversely the intended coordinate coloring supplies an embedding into the decorated host, using fresh vertices for all hard clusters and auxiliary ports. No component bound is needed for this forward embedding.

## Explicit hereditary endpoint class
Fix s>=3 and K>=2, and put L=Ks. S consists of star forests with at most one component of order>s, and that component of order<=L. P consists of linear forests with sum of the orders of components larger than s at most L-1. Let C=S union P. This is hereditary. It has the following finite forbidden family:
1. all trees on L+1 vertices;
2. all cycles of lengths 3,...,L+1;
3. all trees of order<=L which are neither stars nor paths;
4. claw disjoint-union P4;
5. two disjoint copies of K_{1,s};
6. all path forests with every component order at least s+1 and total order in [L,2L-1].
After 1,2 every component is a tree of order<=L. Item 3 makes each a star or a path, and 4 excludes a nonpath star and nonstar path coexisting. For star forests 5 is precisely the condition on the number of large components. For path forests 6 is precisely the total-large-order budget: take the shortest prefix exceeding L-1 to get total less than 2L.
Add the r-part pattern having one edge in each of two distinct parts. The realization construction therefore gives a fixed H whose sufficiently large complete-join free graphs have exactly one active part and decoration in C.

For large m, the maximum internal edge counts in these branches are
E_S(m)=m-1-ceil((m-L)/s),
E_P(m)=m-1-ceil((m-L+1)/s).
These follow by minimizing the number of tree components. A single maximal exceptional component minimizes that number; its subgraphs give all smaller cases needed to attain the formula.

For n divisible by rs, let m0=n/r=ks. At m0 the unique edge-optimal S-decoration is one star of order L and k-K stars of order s, with E0=k(s-1)+K-1. For a shift d in active-part size and optimally balanced other parts, write Q=sum x_i^2. We have Q/2>=|d|. The S edge difference is -Q/2+d-ceil(d/s), strictly negative if d!=0. The P difference is -Q/2+d-ceil((d+1)/s), always negative. Hence the complete-join edge optimum is unique up to isomorphism.

The competitor has active part m0+1, other part sizes m0-1,m0,...,m0, and decoration one P_{L-1}, k-K copies of P_s, and one P2. Its internal count is E0 and it loses exactly one total edge.

## First-order cycle comparison lemma
Let r>=3. Consider two sequences A_n,B_n, each a complete join of r internal graphs of uniformly bounded maximum degree. Part sizes are n/r+x_i with bounded x_i, internal edge counts E_i=alpha_i n/r+O(1), and internal wedges W_i=sum_v binom(d_i(v),2)=beta_i n/r+O(1). Suppose the alpha_i agree between sequences, and E(B)-E(A)=Delta E is constant. Then
n log(c(B)/c(A)) ->
  2r/(r-1) Delta E
 -r/(r-2) (sum (x_i^B)^2-sum (x_i^A)^2)
 +4r/((r-1)(r-2)) sum alpha_i(x_i^B-x_i^A)
 -2r/(r-1)^2 sum (beta_i^B-beta_i^A).

Derivation: enumerate omitted vertices and vertex-disjoint internal paths, orient each nontrivial path, and contract each to one vertex. What remains is a Hamilton cycle in a complete multipartite graph. The fixed-offset Hamilton count obeys
h(n_1,...,n_r)=B_n[1+(a_r-r/(r-2) sum x_i^2)/n+O(n^-2)],
B_n~(n-1)!/2 ((r-1)/r)^n (r/(r-2))^{(r-1)/2}.
This follows by Fourier inversion of the color-count distribution of the stationary Markov chain with transition probabilities 1/(r-1) off the diagonal. Its covariance on the sum-zero subspace is (r-2)/r^2 times the identity; Stirling's formula for prod n_i! changes the Gaussian coefficient to r/(r-2). The ordinary Edgeworth expansion gives the displayed uniform fixed-offset error. For k total contractions/omissions, its ratio is rho^-k n^-k times [1+{k(k+1)/2-kappa(Q'-Q)}/n+O(n^-2 poly(k))].
The leading omission sums are independent Poisson weights with means 1/(r-1) per part; the internal-edge sums have means 2 alpha_i/(r-1). Thus the Q'-Q term yields the stated imbalance cross term. For a graph with E edges and W wedges the generating polynomial of oriented linear forests has logarithm 2Ez-2(E+W)z^2+O(n z^3), with z=r/((r-1)n). Indeed a pair of disjoint edges has weight4, an adjacent pair weight2; all other first-order terms depend only on alpha_i, so cancel in the comparison. This yields the wedge term.
Uniformity of summation follows by restricting total omissions/contractions to O(log n), using the uniform Edgeworth expansion there, and bounding the remaining sum by a factorial tail; for counts >=epsilon n a crude cycle count loses a superexponential factorial factor. This also gives full fixed-order expansions to any order for bounded internal component orders.

For the constructed pair, alpha=(s-1)/s, Q_B-Q_A=2, Delta E=0, and wedge-density advantage is D=(s-2)(s-3)/(2s). The limiting comparison is
Delta(r,s)=-2r/(r-2)+4r(s-1)/(s(r-1)(r-2))+r(s-2)(s-3)/(s(r-1)^2).
In particular Delta(3,s)=3(s^2-5s-2)/(4s), Delta(4,s)=4(s-8)/9,
Delta(r,2r)=-(r-4)/((r-2)(r-1)),
Delta(r,2r+1)=6r/((r-2)(r-1)(2r+1)).
Thus s6 works for r3 and s=2r+1 works for r>=5.

For r4 take s8 and increase K. The first-order comparison is zero. In the second-order coefficient of log(c(P)/c(S)), the difference in wedges of the fixed exceptional gadgets has a positive quadratic K contribution. Specifically W_S-W_P=(s^2/2)K^2+O(K), and its coefficient is 2r^2/(r-1)^2. Every other K-dependent term is at most linear: the internal edge offsets are identical K-1, and bounded-gadget moments beyond wedges enter only at order n^-3. Therefore the second-order coefficient is [r^2 s^2/(r-1)^2]K^2+O(K), positive for some fixed K. Exact checks with K2 already show positivity for n128,256,512,1024, but that finite check is not the proof of eventual positivity.

## What this note does not supply
The global conversion of edge extremizers to complete-join form uses the Simonovits bounded-block theorem plus exceptional-vertex cleanup, discussed separately by agents1/4. The universal lower-threshold half additionally needs a corresponding global structure theorem for cycle maximizers, which is NOT proved by this note.
'''
Path('workspace/agent3_endpoint_notes.md').write_text(text)
print('Wrote endpoint construction and analytic comparison notes:',len(text),'characters')

import networkx as nx,random,itertools as it,json
rng=random.Random(202601)
def matchingone(G):
 for I in nx.find_cliques(nx.complement(G)):
  R=G.subgraph(set(G)-set(I))
  if max(dict(R.degree()).values(),default=0)<=1:return True
 return False

def host(h,kind):
 p=list(range(h));rng.shuffle(p);a=rng.randrange(h//3,2*h//3+1);A=p[:a];B=p[a:];G=nx.Graph();G.add_nodes_from(p);G.add_edges_from(it.product(A,B))
 if kind=='p3':
  for i in range(0,len(A)-2,3):G.add_edges_from([(A[i],A[i+1]),(A[i+1],A[i+2])])
 else:
  for X in [A,B]:G.add_edges_from(zip(X[::2],X[1::2]))
 return G
for h in range(7,19):
 tested=0;found=False
 for trial in range(3000):
  G=nx.intersection(host(h,'p3'),host(h,'match'))
  if nx.is_bipartite(G):continue
  if not any(nx.is_bipartite(G.subgraph(set(G)-{v})) for v in G):continue
  tested+=1
  if not matchingone(G):
   print('COUNTEREXAMPLE',h,list(G.edges()));found=True;break
 print('h',h,'tested',tested,'found',found,flush=True)
 if found:break

```

import sys
sys.path.insert(0,'workspace')
from agent3_branches import cycle_count,mul
from math import comb

def power(p,k):
 q=[1]
 for _ in range(k):q=mul(q,p)
 return q
for k in [5,10,20,40,80,160]:
 m=2*k+1;n=2*m
 S=mul([1,15,9,1],power([1,4,1],k-1))
 T=mul([1,1],power([1,4,1],k))
 ind=[comb(m,j) for j in range(m+1)]
 Bedge=mul([1,4,1],[comb(m-2,j) for j in range(m-1)])
 cs=cycle_count([S,ind],[m,m],1)
 ct=cycle_count([T,Bedge],[m,m],0)
 print(n,float(ct/cs),float((ct-cs)/cs*n*n))

import sys
sys.path.insert(0,'workspace')
from agent3_branches import cycle_count,mul
from math import comb

def power(p,k):
 q=[1]
 for _ in range(k):q=mul(q,p)
 return q

def star(L):
 p=[comb(L,k) for k in range(L+1)]
 for j in range(L-1):p[j+1]+=2*(L-1)*comb(L-2,j)
 for j in range(L-2):p[j+1]+=2*comb(L-1,2)*comb(L-3,j)
 return p
for f in [1,2,3,4]:
 for k in [40,80,160]:
  m=2*k+1;n=2*m;L=4*f+2
  S=mul(mul(star(L),[1,1]),power([1,4,1],(m-L-1)//2))
  T=mul([1,1],power([1,4,1],k))
  ind=[comb(m,j) for j in range(m+1)]
  Bedge=mul(power([1,4,1],f),[comb(m-2*f,j) for j in range(m-2*f+1)])
  cs=cycle_count([S,ind],[m,m],0);ct=cycle_count([T,Bedge],[m,m],0)
  print(f,n,float((ct-cs)/cs*n*n))

```

import networkx as nx,itertools as it,collections

def maxsets(G):return [set(I) for I in nx.find_cliques(nx.complement(G))]
def ismatch(G):return max(dict(G.degree()).values(),default=0)<=1
def p3(G):return all(len(C)<=3 and G.subgraph(C).number_of_edges()==len(C)-1 for C in nx.connected_components(G))
def starorder(R):
 big=[]
 for C in nx.connected_components(R):
  T=R.subgraph(C)
  if len(C)<=2:continue
  if len(C)<3 or T.number_of_edges()!=len(C)-1 or max(dict(T.degree()).values())!=len(C)-1:return 999
  big.append(len(C))
 return big[0] if len(big)==1 else (0 if not big else 999)
rows=[]
for G in nx.graph_atlas_g():
 n=len(G)
 if n<3 or nx.is_bipartite(G) or not any(nx.is_bipartite(G.subgraph(set(G)-{v})) for v in G):continue
 Is=maxsets(G)
 if not any(p3(G.subgraph(set(G)-I)) for I in Is):continue
 if any(ismatch(G.subgraph(set(G)-I)) for I in Is):continue
 D=min(starorder(G.subgraph(set(G)-I)) for I in Is)
 b=999
 for bits in it.product([0,1],repeat=n-1):
  A={0}|{i+1 for i,x in enumerate(bits) if x};B=set(G)-A
  GA=G.subgraph(A);GB=G.subgraph(B)
  if ismatch(GA) and ismatch(GB):b=min(b,GA.number_of_edges(),GB.number_of_edges())
 if b<999:rows.append((D,b,n))
print('count',len(rows),'pairs',collections.Counter((D,b) for D,b,n in rows))
print('max D-2b',max(D-2*b for D,b,n in rows))

```

import networkx as nx,random,itertools as it
rng=random.Random(12345)
def starorder(R):
 big=[]
 for C in nx.connected_components(R):
  T=R.subgraph(C)
  if len(C)<=2:continue
  if T.number_of_edges()!=len(C)-1 or max(dict(T.degree()).values())!=len(C)-1:return 999
  big.append(len(C))
 return big[0] if len(big)==1 else (0 if not big else 999)
def host(h,kind):
 p=list(range(h));rng.shuffle(p);a=rng.randrange(h//3,2*h//3+1);A=p[:a];B=p[a:];G=nx.Graph();G.add_nodes_from(p);G.add_edges_from(it.product(A,B))
 if kind=='p3':
  for i in range(0,len(A)-2,3):G.add_edges_from([(A[i],A[i+1]),(A[i+1],A[i+2])])
 else:
  for X in [A,B]:G.add_edges_from(zip(X[::2],X[1::2]))
 return G
for h in range(8,25):
 tested=0;found=False
 for trial in range(2000):
  G=nx.intersection(host(h,'p3'),host(h,'match'))
  # randomly sparsify slightly
  if trial%3==0:G.remove_edges_from([e for e in G.edges() if rng.random()<.1])
  if nx.is_bipartite(G):continue
  if not any(nx.is_bipartite(G.subgraph(set(G)-{v})) for v in G):continue
  tested+=1
  D=999
  for I in nx.find_cliques(nx.complement(G)):
   D=min(D,starorder(G.subgraph(set(G)-set(I))))
   if D<=3:break
  if D>3:
   print('COUNTEREXAMPLE',h,'D',D,list(G.edges()));found=True;break
 print('h',h,'tested',tested,'found',found,flush=True)
 if found:break

import networkx as nx,random,itertools as it
from z3 import *
rng=random.Random(834938)
def solve(G,one=False):
 x={v:Bool('x'+str(v)) for v in G};s=Solver();s.add(x[0])
 for v in G:
  s.add(PbLe([(x[v]==x[u],1) for u in G[v]],1))
 if one:
  a=[And(x[u],x[v]) for u,v in G.edges()];b=[And(Not(x[u]),Not(x[v])) for u,v in G.edges()]
  s.add(Or(PbLe([(z,1) for z in a],1),PbLe([(z,1) for z in b],1)))
 return s.check()==sat
for n in [8,10,12,15,20,25,30,40]:
 tested=0;found=False
 for trial in range(1000):
  a=(n-1)//2;A=list(range(1,a+1));B=list(range(a+1,n));G=nx.Graph();G.add_nodes_from(range(n))
  p=rng.uniform(.15,.7)
  G.add_edges_from((u,v) for u in A for v in B if rng.random()<p)
  G.add_edges_from((0,v) for v in range(1,n) if rng.random()<rng.uniform(.2,.9))
  if nx.is_bipartite(G) or not solve(G):continue
  tested+=1
  if not solve(G,True):print('COUNTEREXAMPLE',n,list(G.edges()),flush=True);found=True;break
 print(n,tested,found,flush=True)
 if found:break

```

from pathlib import Path
text=r'''# Uniform analytic clone lemma (r>=3)

Fix r>=3 and degree bounds D,B. There is a neighborhood U of u=(1/r,...,1/r) and positive analytic functions theta_i(p), w_ij(p), A(p), such that for any graph G with a partition V_i, p_i=|V_i|/n in U, maximum internal degree <=D, and maximum missing-cross degree <=B,

log c(G) = log h(K_{|V_1|,...,|V_r|}) + A(p)
 + sum_i 2 theta_i(p) E_i/n - sum_{i<j} w_ij(p) M_ij/n + O_{r,D,B}(1/n).    (1)

Here E_i counts internal edges and M_ij missing cross edges. The error is uniform over all such graphs. At u,
theta_i(u)=r/(r-1), w_ij(u)=2r/(r-1), A(u)=r/(r-1).

Proof details. Let Q(z) have entries sqrt(z_i z_j) off the diagonal and zero diagonal; its simple Perron eigenvalue lambda(z) is analytic near z=(1,...,1). On the hyperplane sum log z_i=0, the Hessian of log lambda is the positive definite covariance matrix of the non-looping r-state chain. At z=1 this covariance has eigenvalue (r-2)/r^2 on the sum-zero space. Thus the implicit-function theorem gives an analytic saddle z(p) for p near u. Perron spectral separation on the Fourier torus gives a uniform local central limit/Edgeworth expansion, by Taylor expansion at the saddle and exponentially small tails outside its neighborhood. Consequently
h(n_i)= C(p) n^{-1} n! exp(n Phi(p)) [1+O(1/n)]
with analytic C>0 and Phi, and full expansions with derivatives, uniform on a compact subset of U. On the simplex,
Phi(u)=log((r-1)/r),
D^2 Phi(u)|_{sumzero}= -2r/(r-2) I.
(The latter follows by adding Stirling's sum p_i log p_i to the Legendre transform of log lambda.)
The ratio obtained by deleting one fixed labeled vertex of part i is theta_i(p)/n [1+O(1/n)], where theta_i>0 is analytic. For any k fixed vertex deletions/contractions its ratio is the product of these factors, times 1+O(k^C/n), uniformly for k<=O(log n). The probability of a fixed cross edge ij in a uniformly random Hamilton cycle is w_ij(p)/n[1+O(1/n)], also analytic; w_ij=2q_ij/(p_i p_j), where q_ij is the stationary directed transition frequency in the saddlepoint Markov chain.

Now enumerate cycles by their omitted vertices and maximal internal paths, orienting each nonsingleton internal path, and exclude missing cross edges by inclusion-exclusion. For systems of k vertex-disjoint selected exceptional edges and omitted vertices, the Hamilton-cycle ratio is the product of the one-object factors times 1+O(k^C/n). Systems with overlaps have total weight O(1/n): bounded exceptional degree gives O(n) adjacent pairs, each of weight O(n^-2); a connected cluster of at least two selected edges has at most O(n) embeddings and weight O(n^-2) or smaller. Summing disjoint systems exponentiates:
A(p)=sum_i p_i theta_i(p),
internal contribution sum_i 2 theta_i E_i/n,
missing contribution -sum_{i<j} w_ij M_ij/n.
The sum of absolute exceptional activities is bounded in terms of D+B, so inclusion-exclusion cancellations do not spoil the uniform O(1/n) relative error (the limiting exponential is bounded away from zero). Tails beyond O(log n) are uniformly bounded by factorial tails. For a linear fraction of chosen edges/omissions, the elementary bound binom(n,p) binom(Cn,k) C^k (n-p-k)! relative to n! exp(-O(n)) is superexponentially small; intermediate tails use the saddle ratio bound C^{k+p} n^{-k-p}. This proves (1).

## Moving a vertex between parts
Let F be obtained from G by deleting v's incident edges and making it a pure independent-class clone in part j, whereas v belonged to part i in G. The new partition has n_i-1,n_j+1. Both graphs satisfy the same bounded internal/missing-cross degree conditions. All exceptional edge counts change by O(D+B), and p changes by O(1/n), so the analytic correction in (1) changes by O(1/n). Therefore

log(c(F)/c(G)) = log(h(new parts)/h(old parts)) + O(1/n).       (2)

By symmetry, partial_i Phi-partial_j Phi is divisible analytically by p_i-p_j. Its value divided by p_i-p_j tends to -2r/(r-2) as p->u. Hence shrinking U if necessary,

log(h(new parts)/h(old parts)) >= c_r (n_i-n_j)/n - C_r/n

whenever n_i is a largest and n_j a smallest part. Combining with (2), a cycle maximizer in this class has all part differences bounded by a constant depending only on r,D,B.

The clone operation is H-free whenever G is H-free and n is large: any new embedding must use v, which has no neighbors in its new part. Replace v by a fresh old vertex of part j adjacent to all the at most h(H)-1 required neighbors; at most (h(H)-1)B candidates are excluded by missing cross edges. This yields an embedding into G, contradiction.

Thus after the combinatorial repair lemma supplies bounded internal degree and bounded missing-cross degree, the part sizes are n/r+O_H(1), without assuming bounded imbalance in advance.
'''
Path('workspace/agent3_clone_lemma.md').write_text(text)
print('Wrote analytic clone lemma:',len(text),'characters')

```

import sys
sys.path.insert(0,'workspace')
from agent3_branches import cycle_count,mul
from math import comb

def power(p,k):
 q=[1]
 for _ in range(k):q=mul(q,p)
 return q

def star(L):
 p=[comb(L,k) for k in range(L+1)]
 for j in range(L-1):p[j+1]+=2*(L-1)*comb(L-2,j)
 for j in range(L-2):p[j+1]+=2*comb(L-1,2)*comb(L-3,j)
 return p
for f in [1,2,3,4]:
 for k in [40,80,160]:
  m=2*k+1;n=2*m;L=4*f+1
  S=mul(star(L),power([1,4,1],(m-L)//2))
  T=mul([1,1],power([1,4,1],k))
  ind=[comb(m,j) for j in range(m+1)]
  Bedge=mul(power([1,4,1],f),[comb(m-2*f,j) for j in range(m-2*f+1)])
  cs=cycle_count([S,ind],[m,m],0);ct=cycle_count([T,Bedge],[m,m],0)
  print(f,n,float((ct-cs)/cs*n*n))

```

import sys
sys.path.insert(0,'workspace')
from agent3_branches import cycle_count,mul
from agent3_exact_cycles import brute
from math import comb
import networkx as nx
from pathlib import Path

def power(p,k):
 q=[1]
 for _ in range(k):q=mul(q,p)
 return q

def paths(G):
 es=list(G.edges());n=len(G);p=[0]*(n+1)
 for mask in range(1<<len(es)):
  F=nx.Graph();F.add_edges_from(es[i] for i in range(len(es)) if mask>>i&1)
  if max(dict(F.degree()).values(),default=0)>2 or (len(F) and not nx.is_forest(F)):continue
  k=nx.number_connected_components(F);left=n-len(F)
  for j in range(left+1):p[k+j]+=2**k*comb(left,j)
 return p
p4=paths(nx.complete_graph(4));p5=paths(nx.complete_graph(5))
print('K4 path polynomial',p4,'K5',p5)
for k in [10,20,40,80,160]:
 m=2*k+1;n=2*m
 S_A=mul(p5,power([1,4,1],(m-5)//2))
 S_B=mul([1,4,1],[comb(m-2,j) for j in range(m-1)])
 T_A=mul([1,1],power([1,4,1],k))
 T_B=mul(mul(p4,[1,4,1]),[comb(m-6,j) for j in range(m-5)])
 cs=cycle_count([S_A,S_B],[m,m],brute(nx.complete_graph(5)))
 ct=cycle_count([T_A,T_B],[m,m],brute(nx.complete_graph(4)))
 assert ct>cs
 print('n',n,'T/S',float(ct/cs),'n(T/S-1)',float(n*(ct-cs)/cs))
Path('workspace/agent3_clique_profile_check.py').write_text(Path('scratchpad.py').read_text())

```

import networkx as nx,z3,json,itertools as it
from pathlib import Path
G=nx.node_link_graph(json.load(open('workspace/agent2_glue_b_vs_clique.json')))

def embeds(a,bcliques,missing=(),timeout=30000):
 nlab=2+a+sum(bcliques);Apart={0}|set(range(2,2+a));Bpart=set(range(nlab))-Apart
 blocks={}
 for u in range(2,2+a):blocks[u]=0
 off=2+a
 for i,s in enumerate(bcliques,1):
  for u in range(off,off+s):blocks[u]=i
  off+=s
 miss={frozenset(x) for x in missing}
 adj=[]
 for x in range(nlab):
  for y in range(nlab):
   if x in Apart and y in Bpart or x in Bpart and y in Apart:
    yes=frozenset((x,y)) not in miss
   elif x==y==0:yes=True
   elif x!=y and x in blocks and y in blocks and blocks[x]==blocks[y]:yes=True
   else:yes=False
   if yes:adj.append((x,y))
 S=z3.Solver();S.set(timeout=timeout);vs=list(G);lab={v:z3.Int('l'+str(i)) for i,v in enumerate(vs)}
 for v in G:S.add(lab[v]>=0,lab[v]<nlab)
 for u,v in G.edges():S.add(z3.Or(*[z3.And(lab[u]==x,lab[v]==y) for x,y in adj]))
 for v in G:
  nei=[lab[w]==0 for w in G[v]]
  if nei:S.add(z3.Implies(lab[v]==0,z3.PbLe([(q,1) for q in nei],1)))
 for i in range(2,nlab):S.add(z3.PbLe([(lab[v]==i,1) for v in G],1))
 return str(S.check())

cases=[[(2,6),(2,7)],[(2,6),(3,6)],[(2,6),(3,7)],[(2,10),(2,11)],[(2,10),(3,10)],[(2,10),(3,11)],[(2,6),(2,10)],[(2,6),(3,10)]]
for miss in cases:print(miss,embeds(4,[4,2],miss),flush=True)
Path('workspace/agent3_crossdefect_search.py').write_text(Path('scratchpad.py').read_text())

import networkx as nx,itertools as it,json
from pathlib import Path
Bs=[nx.node_link_graph(x) for x in json.load(open('workspace/agent2_Bmaximal_small.json'))]
Bs.append(nx.disjoint_union(nx.complete_graph(4),nx.path_graph(2)))

def twoP3(F):
 ts=[set(t) for t in it.combinations(F,3) if F.subgraph(t).number_of_edges()>=2]
 return any(a.isdisjoint(b) for a,b in it.combinations(ts,2))
As=[]
for F in nx.graph_atlas_g():
 if len(F)<3 or not nx.is_connected(F) or twoP3(F) or max(dict(F.degree()).values())>=6:continue
 X=F.number_of_edges()-len(F)//2
 if X<2:continue
 As.append(F)
print('A candidates',len(As),flush=True)
count=0;free=[]
for bi,B in enumerate(Bs):
 for A in As:
  X=A.number_of_edges()-len(A)//2;Y=B.number_of_edges()
  if X+Y<9:continue
  res=embeds(len(A),[len(B)],A_edges=A.edges(),B_edges=B.edges())
  count+=1
  if res!='sat':
   row={'A':list(A.edges()),'B':list(B.edges()),'X':X,'Y':Y,'result':res}
   free.append(row);print('POSSIBLE RESCUE',row,flush=True)
 print('B type',bi,'done',flush=True)
print('checks',count,'free',len(free))
Path('workspace/agent3_conditional_matrix_results.json').write_text(json.dumps(free))
Path('workspace/agent3_conditional_matrix.py').write_text(Path('scratchpad.py').read_text())

import networkx as nx,itertools as it,json,z3
from pathlib import Path
G=nx.node_link_graph(json.load(open('workspace/agent2_glue_b_vs_clique.json')))
def embeds(a,bcliques,missing=(),timeout=30000,A_edges=None,B_edges=None):
 nlab=2+a+sum(bcliques);Apart={0}|set(range(2,2+a));Bpart=set(range(nlab))-Apart
 blocks={u:0 for u in range(2,2+a)};off=2+a
 for i,s in enumerate(bcliques,1):
  for u in range(off,off+s):blocks[u]=i
  off+=s
 miss={frozenset(x) for x in missing};adj=[]
 AE=None if A_edges is None else {frozenset(e) for e in A_edges};BE=None if B_edges is None else {frozenset(e) for e in B_edges}
 for x in range(nlab):
  for y in range(nlab):
   if x in Apart and y in Bpart or x in Bpart and y in Apart:yes=frozenset((x,y)) not in miss
   elif x==y==0:yes=True
   elif x!=y and x in blocks and y in blocks and blocks[x]==blocks[y]:
    if blocks[x]==0:yes=AE is None or frozenset((x-2,y-2)) in AE
    else:yes=BE is None or frozenset((x-2-a,y-2-a)) in BE
   else:yes=False
   if yes:adj.append((x,y))
 S=z3.Solver();S.set(timeout=timeout);vs=list(G);lab={v:z3.Int('l'+str(i)) for i,v in enumerate(vs)}
 for v in G:S.add(lab[v]>=0,lab[v]<nlab)
 for u,v in G.edges():S.add(z3.Or(*[z3.And(lab[u]==x,lab[v]==y) for x,y in adj]))
 for v in G:
  nei=[lab[w]==0 for w in G[v]]
  if nei:S.add(z3.Implies(lab[v]==0,z3.PbLe([(q,1) for q in nei],1)))
 for i in range(2,nlab):S.add(z3.PbLe([(lab[v]==i,1) for v in G],1))
 return str(S.check())
```

import sys
sys.path.insert(0,'workspace')
from agent3_branches import cycle_count,mul
from agent3_exact_cycles import brute,join_forests
import networkx as nx
from math import comb,factorial
from itertools import combinations
from pathlib import Path

def poly_mark(G,u):
 n=len(G);es=list(G.edges());p=[0]*(n+1);q=[0]*(n+1)
 for mask in range(1<<len(es)):
  F=nx.Graph();F.add_edges_from(es[i] for i in range(len(es)) if mask>>i&1)
  if max(dict(F.degree()).values(),default=0)>2 or (len(F) and not nx.is_forest(F)):continue
  c=nx.number_connected_components(F);rem=n-len(F);wt=2**c
  for j in range(rem+1):p[c+j]+=wt*comb(rem,j)
  if u in F:
   if F.degree(u)==1:
    for j in range(rem+1):q[c+j]+=wt*comb(rem,j)
  else:
   for j in range(rem):q[c+j+1]+=2*wt*comb(rem-1,j)
 return p,q

def forced_cross(pa,pb):
 ans=sum(pa[l]*pb[l]*factorial(l-1)**2 for l in range(1,min(len(pa),len(pb))))
 assert ans%4==0
 return ans//4-1

def powpoly(p,k):
 q=[1]
 for _ in range(k):q=mul(q,p)
 return q

# Brute-force verification of marked polynomial formula.
checks=0
for a,b in [(2,2),(3,2),(3,3),(4,3)]:
 for FA,FB in [(nx.empty_graph(a),nx.empty_graph(b)),(nx.path_graph(a),nx.empty_graph(b)),(nx.path_graph(a),nx.path_graph(b))]:
  G=join_forests([FA,FB]);base=brute(G)
  for u in FA:
   for v in FB:
    H=G.copy();H.remove_edge(u,a+v)
    pa,qa=poly_mark(FA,u);pb,qb=poly_mark(FB,v)
    x=forced_cross(qa,qb);y=base-brute(H)
    assert x==y,(a,b,u,v,x,y)
    checks+=1
print('Marked cross-edge formula brute verified',checks)

import mpmath as mp
eta=float(mp.besseli(1,2*mp.sqrt(2))/(mp.sqrt(2)*mp.besseli(0,2*mp.sqrt(2))))
print('eta',eta)
for m in [20,40,80,160,320]:
 pa=powpoly([1,4,1],m//2)
 qa=mul([0,4,2],powpoly([1,4,1],m//2-1))
 pb=[comb(m,k) for k in range(m+1)]
 qb=[0]+[2*comb(m-1,k) for k in range(m)]
 c=cycle_count([pa,pb],[m,m]);f=forced_cross(qa,qb)
 scaled=float((f/c-2/m)*m*m)
 print('m',m,'singlehole correction',scaled,'prediction',-4*eta)
Path('workspace/agent3_marked_cross_cycles.py').write_text(Path('scratchpad.py').read_text())

```

import sympy as S
from sympy.functions.combinatorial.numbers import stirling
from pathlib import Path
q,h,delta,E,WA,WB,w=S.symbols('q h delta E WA WB w')

def expansion(d):
 u=q+d
 U=(u*(u-1)+q*(q+1))/2
 V=d*q*(q+1)/2+(u*(u-1)*(2*u-1)+q*(q+1)*(2*q+1))/12
 FF={0:S.Integer(1) if d==0 else h,1:2*h if d==0 else S.Integer(1)}
 for k in range(1,15):FF[k+1]=S.expand(2*FF[k-1]-k*FF[k])
 for k in range(1,15):FF[-k]=FF[k]/2**k
 def T(r,s,P=1):
  P=S.Poly(S.expand(S.sympify(P).subs(q,q+s)),q);ans=0
  for (j,),c in P.terms():
   for ell in range(j+1):ans+=c*stirling(j,ell,kind=2)*FF[d+s-r+ell]
  return S.expand(ans)
 PA=2*delta*w-S.Rational(7,2)*w*w
 QA=(-6*delta-2*WA)*w*w+S.Rational(26,3)*w**3
 PB=2*E*w-S.Rational(1,2)*w*w
 QB=(-6*E-2*WB)*w*w+S.Rational(1,3)*w**3
 def tsum(A,B,P=1):
  ans=0
  for (r,),a in S.Poly(A,w).terms():
   for (s,),b in S.Poly(B,w).terms():ans+=a*b*T(r,s,P)
  return S.expand(ans)
 H1=tsum(PA,1)+tsum(1,PB)+T(0,0,U)
 H2=tsum(QA+PA*PA/2,1)+tsum(PA,PB)+tsum(1,QB+PB*PB/2+d*PB)+tsum(PA,1,U)+tsum(1,PB,U)+T(0,0,V+U*U/2)
 log2=S.expand(H2-H1*H1/2)
 graphlog2=S.expand(log2-log2.subs({delta:0,E:0,WA:0,WB:0}))
 return S.expand(H1),S.factor(graphlog2)
for d in [0,1]:
 x,y=expansion(d)
 print('d',d,'H1=',x,'\ngraphlog2=',S.collect(S.expand(y),[delta,E,WA,WB]))
Path('workspace/agent3_q2_expansion_symbolic.py').write_text(Path('scratchpad.py').read_text())

from pathlib import Path
text=r'''# Exact q=2 finite-profile cycle expansion

Let |A|=m, |B|=b=m-d, with d fixed. Outside finite gadgets J_A,J_B, A is a perfect matching and B is independent; all missing cross edges form a fixed bipartite graph R on gadget vertices. Absorb a matching edge into J_A if necessary to ensure every hole endpoint lies in a gadget. Put
 delta=e(J_A)-v(J_A)/2, E=e(J_B),
 W_A=sum binom(deg_JA(u),2), W_B analogously,
 M=e(R), W_R=sum binom(deg_R(u),2),
 L_A=sum_{u in A} deg_JA(u)deg_R(u), L_B analogously.
All quantities are fixed while m tends to infinity in the appropriate parity class.

Define F_k=[z^k] exp(2z+z^{-1})=2^{k/2} I_{|k|}(2sqrt2). Then
 F_{k+1}=2F_{k-1}-kF_k, F_{-k}=2^{-k}F_k.
The leading cycle count is
 c(G) ~ m! b!/(2b) F_d.
For even total n, the unique leading-optimal imbalance is d=0; for odd n it is d=1. Indeed F_2/F_0=2-2h0<1, F_3/F_1=4-4h1<1; larger same-parity positive indices decrease by the termwise bound F_{k+2}/F_k<=4/((k+1)(k+2)), and negative indices are smaller by F_{-k}=2^{-k}F_k.
Here h0=F_{-1}/F_0=0.5631786198117114... and h1=F_0/F_1=0.8878177942322558...=1/(2h0).

For d=0 or1, write h=F_{d-1}/F_d and theta=F_{d+1}/F_d=2h-d. Up to terms independent of the finite profile,
 log c(G)=log(m!b!F_d/(2b))+ H1/m + Q/m^2+O(m^{-3}).
(The displayed H1 includes its universal constant; Q below omits its universal constant.)

For d=0:
 H1=2h delta+4h E-2M+(11h-3)/4,
 Q=(1-h-2h^2)delta^2+(4-4h-8h^2)E^2+(4-8h^2)delta E
   +(5/2-h/2-11h^2/2)delta+(-4+8h-11h^2)E
   +(h-1)W_A+4(h-1)W_B+Q_R.
For d=1:
 H1=2h delta+(4h-2)E-2M+2h-7/4,
 Q=(1-2h^2)delta^2+(6-8h^2)E^2+(4+4h-8h^2)delta E
   +(7/4+2h-4h^2)delta+(-31/2+24h-8h^2)E
   -W_A+8(h-1)W_B+Q_R.
In both cases
 Q_R=2h L_A+2theta L_B+2(theta-h-1)M-2W_R.

## Derivation without holes
Let p_F(z) count collections of vertex-disjoint internal paths on an arbitrary selected vertex subset, marking the number of path blocks by z and giving every nonsingleton path its two orientations. Set R_F(z)=z^{v(F)}p_F(1/z). The first coefficients are
 [z^0]R_F=1,
 [z^1]R_F=v+2e,
 [z^2]R_F=binom(v,2)+2e(v-2)+2e(e-1)-2W.
These identities hold for all finite graphs: selected cycles need at least three edges and do not affect these coefficients.
For the whole A-part (m-a)/2 free matching edges remain, so
 R_A(z)=(1+4z+z^2)^{(m-a)/2}R_JA(z).
On rescaling z=w/m, its logarithm is
 2w + [2delta w-(7/2)w^2]/m
    +[(-6delta-2W_A)w^2+(26/3)w^3]/m^2+O(m^-3).
Similarly
 log R_B(w/b)=w+[2Ew-w^2/2]/b
   +[(-6E-2W_B)w^2+w^3/3]/b^2+O(b^-3).

The exact alternating-block cycle formula is
 c(G)=1/2 sum_{ell>=1} [z^ell]p_A [z^ell]p_B ell!(ell-1)! - mb/2
       + (cycles wholly internal).
The correction terms are negligible to every relative asymptotic order. Set q=b-ell, u=q+d. Relative to m!b!/(2b), the factorial factor, after removing m^{-u}b^{-q}, has logarithm
 U(q,d)/m+V(q,d)/m^2+O(poly(q)/m^3),
 U=(u(u-1)+q(q+1))/2,
 V=d q(q+1)/2+[u(u-1)(2u-1)+q(q+1)(2q+1)]/12.
Combine these three formal expansions and sum coefficients. All sums reduce exactly to F_k because
 sum_q 2^{q+d-r}/((q+d-r)!(q-s)!)=F_{d+s-r},
and factorial moments of q shift the F-index. The symbolic code agent3_q2_expansion_symbolic.py performs these finite polynomial reductions. Factorial tails justify termwise expansion uniformly to any fixed order.

## Cross holes, using marked path polynomials
For u in F let q_F^u(z) count the same oriented path systems, additionally weighted by the number of external cross stubs at u: 0 if omitted or internal in its path, 1 if an endpoint of a nonsingleton path, 2 if a singleton. Its reversed polynomial satisfies
 z^v q_F^u(1/z)/R_F(z)=2[1-(1+deg_F(u))z+O(z^2)].
For a fixed cross edge uv the EXACT forced-edge cycle count is
 1/4 sum_{ell>=1} [z^ell]q_A^u [z^ell]q_B^v ((ell-1)!)^2 -1.
The subtraction removes the spurious 2-cycle on u,v. This identity was independently checked against brute-force cycle DFS on93 joined-forest examples.
Applying the first-order reversed expansions to this formula gives
 P(uv in C)=2/m+[2d-2h(1+deg_JA(u))-2theta deg_JB(v)]/m^2+O(m^-3).
For two prescribed cross edges, the probability both occur is 4/m^2+O(m^-3) if disjoint and 2/m^2+O(m^-3) if incident. Inclusion-exclusion over the fixed hole set, followed by taking logarithms, gives exactly Q_R above. Three or more holes affect only order m^-3.

## Irrationality and the precise exchange target
The Bessel recurrence gives
 h0=1/(1+2/(2+2/(3+2/(4+...)))).
Its positive generalized continued-fraction convergents have denominators q_j>=j!, and errors less than 2^j/(q_j q_{j+1}). The infinite value differs strictly from every convergent, while q_j times the error tends to zero; hence h0 is irrational (otherwise distance from its fixed rational value to the jth convergent is at least1/(Q q_j)). Therefore h1=1/(2h0) is irrational too.

The edge-profile objective is Z=delta+E-M. Put L=delta+2E.
For even n the first-order cycle objective is 2h0 L-2M. Ties force L and M separately equal; on such a tie, maximizing edges means minimizing E. Up to an additive constant depending on L,M, the second-order cycle objective becomes
 -8h0 E^2+[4h0 L+9(h0-1)]E
 +(h0-1)(W_A+4W_B)+2h0(L_A+2L_B)-2W_R.
For odd n the first-order cycle objective is 2h1 L-2(E+M). Ties force L and E+M separately equal, hence Z=L-(E+M) is equal: odd-n ties cannot sacrifice edges.

This does NOT prove the required H-specific exchange theorem. It identifies exactly which linear and quadratic functionals that theorem must control.

## Regression check
For even n=4k+2 compare primary Star9+matching, secondary independent, with primary matching+isolate and two secondary matching edges. Their first-order cycle objectives tie, whereas the latter has two fewer edges. The exact quadratic formula gives
 lim n^2(c_T/c_S-1)=40-56h0=8.4619972905...>0,
agreeing with exact counts for n162,322,642. Thus no arbitrary-hereditary-profile version of the missing H-specific exchange theorem is possible.
'''
Path('workspace/agent3_q2_profile_expansion.md').write_text(text)
print('Wrote exact q2 finite-profile expansion:',len(text),'characters')

import networkx as nx,z3,json,itertools as it
from pathlib import Path
G=nx.node_link_graph(json.load(open('workspace/agent2_glue_b_vs_clique.json')))

def audit(A,B,holes=False):
 a=len(A);b=len(B);L=2+a+b
 edges=set()
 for x,y in it.product(range(L),repeat=2):
  ax=x==0 or 2<=x<2+a;ay=y==0 or 2<=y<2+a
  if ax!=ay:
   if not(holes and x>=2 and y>=2):edges.add((x,y))
  elif x==y==0:edges.add((x,y))
  elif 2<=x<2+a and 2<=y<2+a and A.has_edge(x-2,y-2):edges.add((x,y))
  elif x>=2+a and y>=2+a and B.has_edge(x-2-a,y-2-a):edges.add((x,y))
 S=z3.Solver();vs=list(G);X={v:z3.Int('x'+str(i)) for i,v in enumerate(vs)}
 for v in G:S.add(X[v]>=0,X[v]<L)
 for u,v in G.edges():S.add(z3.Or(*[z3.And(X[u]==x,X[v]==y) for x,y in edges]))
 for v in G:
  if G.degree(v):S.add(z3.Implies(X[v]==0,z3.PbLe([(X[w]==0,1) for w in G[v]],1)))
 for j in range(2,L):S.add(z3.PbLe([(X[v]==j,1) for v in G],1))
 res=S.check()
 if res!=z3.sat:return str(res),None
 model=S.model();lab={v:model.eval(X[v]).as_long() for v in G}
 # Build a genuine finite simple host and injective embedding.
 H=nx.Graph();AC=[];BC=[]
 for k in range(len(G)):
  u=('pair',k,0);v=('pair',k,1);AC +=[u,v];H.add_edge(u,v)
 for k in range(len(G)):BC.append(('isolatedB',k));H.add_node(BC[-1])
 for u in A:H.add_node(('A',u));AC.append(('A',u))
 for u in B:H.add_node(('B',u));BC.append(('B',u))
 H.add_edges_from((('A',u),('A',v)) for u,v in A.edges());H.add_edges_from((('B',u),('B',v)) for u,v in B.edges())
 H.add_edges_from((u,v) for u in AC for v in BC if not(holes and u[0]=='A' and v[0]=='B'))
 M={};k=0
 for C in nx.connected_components(G.subgraph([v for v in G if lab[v]==0])):
  assert len(C)<=2
  for j,v in enumerate(C):M[v]=('pair',k,j)
  k+=1
 k=0
 for v in G:
  if lab[v]==1:M[v]=('isolatedB',k);k+=1
  elif 2<=lab[v]<2+a:M[v]=('A',lab[v]-2)
  elif lab[v]>=2+a:M[v]=('B',lab[v]-2-a)
 assert len(M)==len(G) and len(set(M.values()))==len(G)
 assert all(H.has_edge(M[u],M[v]) for u,v in G.edges())
 return 'sat',{'map':[[list(u),list(v)] for u,v in M.items()],'host_edges':len(H.edges()),'host_vertices':len(H)}

print('H size',len(G),len(G.edges()),'bipartite',nx.is_bipartite(G),'critical',[v for v in G if nx.is_bipartite(G.subgraph(set(G)-{v}))],flush=True)

content:

from pathlib import Path
text=r'''# H47 diagnostic: proof of the n/4 extremal upper bound without Simonovits D

This note concerns the fixed graph H from agent2_glue_b_vs_clique.json. The following facts suffice:
(A) H has chromatic number3 and H-v is bipartite for a vertex v.
(B) H embeds in the complete join of an independent set with sufficiently many disjoint P3's.
(C) H embeds in the complete join of two sufficiently large matchings (isolates may be added).
(D) The complete join of a matching and an independent set is H-free.
The actual finite embeddings for A--C have been checked separately; D follows, for example, from the independently certified larger H-free S-host.

## High-minimum-degree structural lemma
For every fixed constant C, an H-free graph G on n vertices with delta(G)>=n/2-C has, for sufficiently large n, a partition A,B such that
 |A|,|B|=n/2+O_{H,C}(1),
 max internal degree=O_H(1),
 max missing-cross degree=O_{H,C}(1),
 and e(G)<=t2(n)+n/4+O_{H,C}(1).

Proof. By ordinary Erdos--Simonovits stability, e(G)>=n^2/4-O_C(n) gives an initial near-balanced bipartition with o(n^2) internal edges and o(n^2) missing cross edges. Remove o(n) bad vertices, leaving a core whose maximum missing-cross degree is o(n). Every removed vertex has o(n) neighbors in one core part: otherwise it sees at least eta n vertices in both core parts for some eta>0, and these neighborhoods contain a fixed complete bipartite graph K_{h,h}, because every core vertex misses o(n) neighbors across the core partition. Together with that vertex, K_{h,h} contains H by (A). Assign the removed vertex to a core part where its degree is o(n). All new part sizes are n/2+o(n), and every vertex has o(n) internal degree. The lower degree bound then makes every vertex's missing-cross degree o(n).

By (A), H embeds into a complete bipartite graph with a fixed star added to one side (place the critical vertex at the center, and use a proper2-coloring of H-v). Hence an internal star of sufficiently large fixed order in either part, all of whose vertices now have o(n) cross defects, extends greedily to H. Therefore the maximum internal degree is bounded by a constant D_H.
If m is the larger part, any vertex there has degree at most n-m+D_H, so m<=n/2+C+D_H. Thus both part sizes differ from n/2 by a bounded constant. The degree lower bound now yields a bounded missing-cross degree B_{H,C}.

By (B), the internal graph in either part cannot contain more than a fixed number p_H of vertex-disjoint P3's. Indeed the other H-vertices can be chosen greedily in the opposite part, avoiding the bounded cross nonneighbors of the O_H(1) fixed vertices. Remove the vertices of a maximal P3-packing in each part; at most6p_H vertices are removed. Their internal incident edges are O_H(1), since internal degree is bounded. The remaining internal graphs are matchings.
If both matchings have at least a sufficiently large fixed number q_{H,C} of edges, choose the finitely many required edges in one part, and then choose the required matching edges in the other part avoiding their bounded cross nonneighbors. Fill isolated positions greedily. This embeds H by (C), a contradiction. Consequently only one matching can have unbounded size. The total number of internal edges is at most half of one part size plus O_{H,C}(1), namely n/4+O_{H,C}(1). Adding at most |A||B| cross edges proves the displayed upper bound.

## Progressive-record argument
Put g(n)=ex(n,H)-t2(n)-n/4. If g were unbounded above, it would have arbitrarily large record values. At a strict record n, every extremal G satisfies
 delta(G)>=ex(n,H)-ex(n-1,H)>floor(n/2)+1/4,
so delta(G)>=floor(n/2)+1. Apply the high-minimum-degree structural lemma with C=1. Its constant is uniform over these record graphs, giving g(n)<=C_H, contrary to unbounded record values. Hence there is a constant C_H with
 ex(n,H)<=t2(n)+n/4+C_H
for every sufficiently large n (and after increasing C_H, for every n).

By (D), a balanced complete bipartite graph with a maximum matching in one part is H-free, so ex(n,H)>=t2(n)+n/4-O(1). Combining lower and upper bounds shows EVERY edge extremizer has delta(G)>=n/2-O_H(1). Therefore the structural lemma applies to every edge extremizer, not merely to one chosen Simonovits extremizer.

Finally its abundant matching has size n/4-O(1). The actual H47 embedding A2P3 + matching-fillers, B independent, therefore implies each full part has P3-packing number at most1: any two P3's can be used in the finite embedding, and the matching fillers can be chosen from the abundant matching avoiding all bounded cross defects. Thus there is at most one nonmatching connected internal component in each part. The remaining, stronger actual embeddings give the degree and branch exclusions used in the counterdiagnostic. This part needs no cycle-maximizer structural theorem whatsoever.

## Why this matters
If the positive embedding certificates and the independently verified S/T-free certificates are sound, a failure to apply the Simonovits D-theorem cannot save the prescribed S2 answer: the exact n/4 slope upper bound follows from standard Turan stability and the above elementary argument alone.
'''
Path('workspace/agent3_H47_progressive_upper.md').write_text(text)
print('Wrote progressive-record upper-bound proof',len(text))

```

All graphs are finite, simple, and undirected. A graph is $H$-free if it contains no subgraph, not necessarily induced, isomorphic to $H$. For a fixed graph $H$, define
\[
\operatorname{ex}(n,H)=\max\{e(G):|V(G)|=n,\ G\text{ is }H\text{-free}\},
\qquad
\operatorname{cyc}(n,H)=\max\{c(G):|V(G)|=n,\ G\text{ is }H\text{-free}\}.
\]
Let $\chi(H)$ denote the minimum number of colors in a proper vertex coloring of $H$. Let $T_{n,r}$ be the complete $r$-partite graph on $n$ vertices whose part sizes differ by at most one, allowing empty parts, and put $t_r(n)=e(T_{n,r})$.

For each integer $r\ge2$, let $\mathcal S_r$ be the set of real numbers $a\ge0$ for which there exist a fixed graph $H$ with $\chi(H)=r+1$, a constant $C\ge0$, and a positive integer $N_0$ satisfying both conditions:

1. For every integer $N\ge N_0$,
\[
\operatorname{ex}(N,H)\le t_r(N)+aN+C.
\]

\[
e(G)=\operatorname{ex}(n,H)
\quad\text{and}\quad
c(G)=\operatorname{cyc}(n,H).
\]

The choices of $H,C,N_0$ may depend on $r$ and $a$, but not on $N$ or $n$. Determine $\mathcal S_r$ explicitly for every integer $r\ge2$, including whether its finite boundary points belong to it.

content:

from pathlib import Path
import ast
for p in sorted(Path('workspace').glob('agent3*.py')):
 text=p.read_text(); lines=text.splitlines(True); kept=[];removed=[]
 for line in lines:
  if 'scratchpad.py' in line or ('workspace/agent3' in line and '.write_text(source)' in line):removed.append(line.rstrip())
  else:kept.append(line)
 new=''.join(kept);ast.parse(new)
 if removed:p.write_text(new);print(p.name,'REMOVED',removed)
print('All agent3 Python files parse after export-artifact cleanup.')

```
